Since 1999 Scotland has had a devolved national government and parliament with wide-ranging powers over any matter that has not been specifically [reserved](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reserved_and_excepted_matters) to the UK Parliament.[^cite_note-Scottish_Parliament-227][^cite_note-228] Scotland has the most devolved powers of any of the three devolved parliaments in the United Kingdom, with full legislative control over [education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Scotland), [law and order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scots_law), [the economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Scotland), [healthcare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_in_Scotland), [elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_Scotland), [Crown Estate Scotland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Estate_Scotland), the [planning system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planning_system_in_Scotland) and [housing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housing_in_Scotland).[^cite_note-229]&#x20;

Additional powers were transferred to the Scottish Parliament via the [Scotland Act 2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scotland_Act_2012) and the [Scotland Act 2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scotland_Act_2016), such as [some taxation powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxation_in_Scotland), including full control of [income tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Income_tax_in_Scotland) on income earned through employment, [Land and Buildings Transaction Tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_and_Buildings_Transaction_Tax), [Landfill Tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scottish_Landfill_Tax), Aggregates Levy, [Air Departure Tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_Departure_Tax) and [Revenue Scotland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revenue_Scotland), as well as aspects of the [energy network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_Scotland), including [renewable energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_energy_in_Scotland), energy efficiency and onshore [oil and gas licensing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_industry_in_Scotland).[^cite_note-Devolved_Matters_in_Scotland-230][^cite_note-Scottish_Parliament-227] Their power over economic issues is significantly constrained by an [act of the UK Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Kingdom_Internal_Market_Act_2020) passed in 2020.[^cite_note-UKIM-222]&#x20;

The [Scottish Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scottish_Government) is a [Scottish National Party](28887.md) (SNP) [minority government](271434.md),[^cite_note-238] led by the [first minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Minister_of_Scotland), currently [John Swinney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Swinney), the [leader of the SNP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leader_of_the_SNP). In 2014 the [Scottish independence referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Scottish_independence_referendum) was held, with 55.3 per cent voting against independence from the United Kingdom and 44.7 per cent voting in favour, resulting in Scotland staying within the United Kingdom. [Local government in Scotland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_government_in_Scotland) is divided into [32 council areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subdivisions_of_Scotland) with a wide variation in size and population. Local councils are made up of elected councillors, of whom there are 1,223.[^cite_note-political-studies-association-209]&#x20;

The Scottish Parliament is separate from the Scottish Government. It is made up of 129 elected [members of the Scottish Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Members_of_the_Scottish_Parliament) (MSPs). It is the law-making body of Scotland, and thus it scrutinises the work of the incumbent Scottish Government and considers any piece of proposed legislation through parliamentary debates, committees and parliamentary questions.[^cite_note-Devolved_Matters_in_Scotland-230]&#x20;

#### Wales

Since 1999 Wales has had a devolved national government and legislature, known as the [Senedd](238480.md). Elections to the Senedd used the [additional member system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Additional_member_system) until 2026 when the voting system was changed to [closed list](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Closed_list) proportional representation. The number of elected members also increased from 60 to 96, representing 16 [six-member constituencies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senedd_constituencies). The Senedd has more limited powers than those devolved to Scotland.[^cite_note-239] It can legislate on any matter not specifically reserved to the UK Parliament by [Acts of Senedd Cymru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acts_of_Senedd_Cymru). The [Welsh Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welsh_Government) is currently a [Plaid Cymru](53343.md) minority government led by the [first minister](602076.md), [Rhun ap Iorwerth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhun_ap_Iorwerth). [Local government in Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_government_in_Wales) consists of 22 unitary authorities, each led by a leader and cabinet elected by the council itself.&#x20;

#### Northern Ireland

The devolved form of government in Northern Ireland is based on the 1998 [Good Friday Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Good_Friday_Agreement), which brought to an end a 30-year period of [unionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unionism_in_Ireland)-[nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_nationalism) communal conflict known as [the Troubles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Troubles). The Agreement was [confirmed by referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_Northern_Ireland_Good_Friday_Agreement_referendum) and implemented later that year. It established [power sharing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_sharing) arrangements for a devolved government and legislature, referred to as the [Northern Ireland Executive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Ireland_Executive) and the [Northern Ireland Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Ireland_Assembly) respectively.[^cite_note-240] Elections to the Assembly use the [single transferable vote](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Single_transferable_vote) system. The Executive and Assembly have powers similar to those devolved to Scotland.[^cite_note-241] The Executive is led by a [diarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diarchy) representing [unionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Designated_Unionist) and [nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Designated_Nationalist) members of the Assembly.[^cite_note-northernireland1-242] The [First Minister and Deputy First Minister of Northern Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Minister_and_Deputy_First_Minister_of_Northern_Ireland) are the joint heads of government of Northern Ireland.[^cite_note-243][^cite_note-244] [Local government in Northern Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_government_in_Northern_Ireland) since 2015 has been divided between 11 councils with limited responsibilities.[^cite_note-Devenport-211]&#x20;

### Foreign relations

Main article: [Foreign relations of the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_the_United_Kingdom)



UK prime minister Keir Starmer and US president [Donald Trump](4848272.md) shaking hands in a joint press conference in 2025. The UK and the US share a "[Special Relationship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Relationship)".

The United Kingdom is [a permanent member](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Five_\(United_Nations\)) of the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) and a member of [NATO](21133.md), [AUKUS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AUKUS), the Commonwealth of Nations, the [G7](1196634.md), the [G20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G-20_major_economies), the [OECD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_for_Economic_Co-operation_and_Development), the [WTO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization), the [Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe) and the [OSCE](22796.md).[^cite_note-245] It maintains the [British Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Council), an organisation specialising in international cultural and educational opportunities in over 100 countries. The UK remains a [great power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_power) with considerable political, cultural, economic and military influence.[^cite_note-PaulWirtz2004-246][^cite_note-247]&#x20;

The United Kingdom is said to have a "[Special Relationship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Relationship)" with the United States and a close partnership with France – the "[Entente cordiale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entente_cordiale)" – and shares nuclear weapons technology with both countries;[^cite_note-248][^cite_note-249] the [Anglo-Portuguese Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglo-Portuguese_Alliance) is considered to be the oldest binding military alliance in the world. The UK is also closely linked with the Republic of Ireland; the two countries share a [Common Travel Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_Travel_Area) and co-operate through the [British-Irish Intergovernmental Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British-Irish_Intergovernmental_Conference) and the [British-Irish Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British-Irish_Council). Britain's global presence and influence is further amplified through its trading relations, foreign investments, [official development assistance](1316305.md) and military engagements.[^cite_note-250]&#x20;

### Military

Main articles: [British Armed Forces](31730.md) and [Military history of the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_history_of_the_United_Kingdom)



&#x20; Overseas military installations of the United Kingdom, and locally raised units of the [British Overseas Territories](207114.md)

&#x20; Military interventions since 2000: [Palliser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_military_intervention_in_the_Sierra_Leone_Civil_War) (Sierra Leone); [Herrick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Herrick) (Afghanistan); [Enduring Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Enduring_Freedom_%E2%80%93_Horn_of_Africa) (Horn of Africa); [Telic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Telic) (Iraq); [Ellamy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Ellamy) (Libya); and [Shader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Shader) (Islamic State of Iraq and the Levant).

The British Armed Forces consist of three professional service branches: the [Royal Navy](26061.md) and [Royal Marines](93985.md) (forming the [Naval Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naval_Service_\(United_Kingdom\))), the [British Army](4887.md) and the [Royal Air Force](25679.md).[^cite_note-251] The armed forces of the United Kingdom are managed by the [Ministry of Defence](203793.md) and controlled by the [Defence Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defence_Council_of_the_United_Kingdom), chaired by the [Secretary of State for Defence](302332.md). The [Commander-in-Chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commander-in-Chief) is the [British monarch](47063.md), to whom members of the forces swear an oath of allegiance.[^cite_note-252]&#x20;

The armed forces are charged with protecting the United Kingdom and its overseas territories, promoting the UK's global security interests and supporting international peacekeeping efforts. They are active and regular participants in [NATO](21133.md) (including the [Allied Rapid Reaction Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allied_Rapid_Reaction_Corps)), the [Five Power Defence Arrangements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Five_Power_Defence_Arrangements), [RIMPAC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RIMPAC) and other worldwide coalition operations. [Overseas garrisons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overseas_military_bases_of_the_United_Kingdom) and facilities are maintained in [Ascension Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RAF_Ascension_Island), [Bahrain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mina_Salman), [Belize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_of_Belize), [Brunei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_Forces_based_in_Brunei), [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Army_Training_Unit_Suffield), [Cyprus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Forces_Cyprus), [Diego Garcia](8630.md), the [Falkland Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_of_the_Falkland_Islands), [Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Forces_Germany), [Gibraltar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Forces_Gibraltar), [Kenya](48917681.md), [Oman](22316.md), [Qatar](3830783.md) and [Singapore](27318.md).[^cite_note-253]&#x20;

According to the [Stockholm International Peace Research Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stockholm_International_Peace_Research_Institute)[^cite_note-254] and the [International Institute for Strategic Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Institute_for_Strategic_Studies),[^cite_note-255] the UK had the world's [sixth- or fifth-highest military expenditures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_military_expenditures) in 2025. Total defence spending in 2024 was estimated at 2.3 per cent of [gross domestic product](12594.md).[^cite_note-256] Following the end of the [Cold War](325329.md), defence policy has a stated assumption that "the most demanding operations" will be undertaken as part of a coalition.[^cite_note-257]&#x20;

### Law and criminal justice

Main articles: [Law of the United Kingdom](77750.md) and [Crime in the United Kingdom](6130706.md)

Further information: [English law](85441.md), [Northern Ireland law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Ireland_law), and [Scots law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scots_law)



The [Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_the_United_Kingdom) is the final court of appeal for England, Wales and Northern Ireland, and for Scottish civil cases.

The United Kingdom does not have a single legal system as the [1706 Treaty of Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Union) provided for the continuation of Scotland's separate legal system.[^cite_note-Acts_of_Union_1707-34] The UK has three distinct [systems of law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_systems_of_the_world): English law, Northern Ireland law and Scots law. A new [Supreme Court of the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_the_United_Kingdom) came into being in October 2009 to replace the [Appellate Committee of the House of Lords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judicial_functions_of_the_House_of_Lords).[^cite_note-258] The [Judicial Committee of the Privy Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judicial_Committee_of_the_Privy_Council), including the same members as the Supreme Court, is the highest court of appeal for several independent Commonwealth countries, the [British Overseas Territories](207114.md) and the [Crown Dependencies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Dependencies).[^cite_note-259]&#x20;

Both English law, which applies in England and Wales, and Northern Ireland law are based on [common law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_law) (or [case law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Case_law)) principles.[^cite_note-260] It originated in England in the [Middle Ages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Ages) and is the basis for many legal systems around the world.[^cite_note-261] The [courts of England and Wales](240527.md) are headed by the [Senior Courts of England and Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senior_Courts_of_England_and_Wales), consisting of the [Court of Appeal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_of_Appeal_of_England_and_Wales), the [High Court of Justice](26569402.md) for civil cases and the [Crown Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Court) for criminal cases.[^cite_note-262] Scots law is a hybrid system based on common-law and [civil-law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_law_\(legal_system\)) principles. The chief courts are the [Court of Session](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_of_Session), for civil cases,[^cite_note-263] and the [High Court of Justiciary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Court_of_Justiciary), for criminal cases.[^cite_note-264] The Supreme Court of the United Kingdom serves as the highest court of appeal for civil cases under Scots law.[^cite_note-265] The criminal justice system has been characterised by low [conviction rates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conviction_rate) and [court delays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justice_delayed_is_justice_denied) for some crime types.[^cite_note-s576-266]&#x20;

Crime in England and Wales increased between 1981 and 1995. Since that peak there has been an overall fall of 66% in recorded crime from 1995 to 2015,[^cite_note-267] according to [UK crime statistics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crime_statistics_in_the_United_Kingdom).&#x20;

[UK labour laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Kingdom_labour_law) establish employment rights including [a minimum wage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Minimum_Wage_Act_1998), a minimum of 28 days annual holiday, parental leave, statutory sick pay and [a pension](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pensions_Act_2008). [Same-sex marriage](92656.md) has been legal in England, Scotland, and Wales since 2014, and in [Northern Ireland](21265.md) since 2020.[^cite_note-268] [LGBT equality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_the_United_Kingdom) in the United Kingdom is considered advanced by modern standards.[^cite_note-269][^cite_note-270]&#x20;

Since leaving the [EU](9317.md) most disputes under UK-EU agreements are addressed through consultation between the parties. If consultation fails to resolve the issue, either party can request [arbitration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arbitration), typically at the [PCA](38058.md) in [The Hague](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_Palace).[^cite_note-commission.europa.eu-138][^cite_note-271][^cite_note-272] The [EU–UK Trade and Cooperation Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EU%E2%80%93UK_Trade_and_Cooperation_Agreement) states that the UK and EU have to cooperate and negotiate with each other with 'full mutual respect and good faith', as defined by international law.[^cite_note-273] Under the [Windsor Framework](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Windsor_Framework), [Northern Ireland](21265.md) matters requiring interpretation of EU law go to the [ECJ](87187.md), though the [Stormont Brake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stormont_Brake) can prevent new EU rules from taking effect.&#x20;

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_the_United_Kingdom)

Further information: [Economy of England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_England), [Economy of Scotland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Scotland), [Economy of Northern Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Northern_Ireland), and [Economy of Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Wales)



[City of London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_London) skyline from at [Bank Junction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_Junction). [London](17867.md) is Europe's largest financial centre.

[^cite_note-274]

The United Kingdom has a highly developed [social market economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_market_economy).[^cite_note-275][^cite_note-ftms-276] With an estimated nominal [gross domestic product](12594.md) (GDP) of £2.819 trillion in 2025,[^cite_note-IMFWEO.UK-17] it is the [fifth-largest national economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(nominal\)) in the world and the [second-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_sovereign_states_in_Europe_by_GDP_\(nominal\)) in Europe. Its currency, the [pound sterling](270673.md), is the [fourth-most-traded currency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_exchange_market#Most_traded_currencies_by_value) in the [foreign exchange market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_exchange_market) and the world's fourth-largest [reserve currency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reserve_currency), after the [United States dollar](18717338.md), the [euro](9472.md) and the [yen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yen).[^cite_note-277] The pound sterling maintains its high nominal value through both its long history of stability and by never undergoing formal [redenomination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redenomination). Since 2022 the UK has been both the world's [fourth-largest exporter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_exports)[^cite_note-278] and [fourth-largest importer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_imports)[^cite_note-279] of goods and services. Despite having one of the highest levels of [income inequality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Income_inequality_in_the_United_Kingdom) in the [OECD](33853117.md),[^cite_note-280][^cite_note-281] the UK has a very high [HDI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Development_Index) ranking, including when [adjusted for inequality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_inequality-adjusted_Human_Development_Index). As of 2025 the [UK unemployment rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unemployment_in_the_United_Kingdom) is 4.7%[^cite_note-282] and the annual [real GDP per head growth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_real_GDP_per_capita_growth) was 1.1%.[^cite_note-f919-283]&#x20;

[HM Treasury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HM_Treasury), led by the [Chancellor of the Exchequer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chancellor_of_the_Exchequer), is responsible for developing and executing the government's [public finance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_finance) and [economic policy](210522.md). The [Department for Business and Trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_for_Business_and_Trade) is responsible for business, international trade, and enterprise. The [Bank of England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_England) is the UK's [central bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_bank) and responsible for issuing notes and coins in the pound sterling. Banks in Scotland and Northern Ireland retain the right to issue their own notes, subject to retaining enough Bank of England notes in reserve to cover their issue.&#x20;

### Industries and services



The [Bank of England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_England) is the [central bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_bank) of the United Kingdom and the model on which most modern central banks have been based.



[Concorde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concorde) was a [supersonic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supersonic_speed) airliner that reduced transatlantic flight time from 8 hours to 3.5 hours.

[^cite_note-284]

The [service sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tertiary_sector_of_the_economy) made up around 80 per cent of the UK's [GVA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_value_added) in 2023.[^cite_note-285] As of 2023 it is the world's [second-largest exporter of services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_service_exports)[^cite_note-286] and in 2024 was the world's largest net exporter of [financial services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_services_in_the_United_Kingdom).[^cite_note-287] [London](17867.md) is the world capital for foreign exchange trading, with a market share of 37.8 per cent in 2022 of the global turnover.[^cite_note-288] It is the largest urban economy in Europe[^cite_note-289] and, alongside [New York](645042.md), the city in the world most integrated with the [global economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_economy).[^cite_note-290] London is also one of the world's leading financial centres, ranking second in the 2025 [Global Financial Centres Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Financial_Centres_Index).\[^cite_note-[www.longfinance.net-291](http://www.longfinance.net-291/)] [Edinburgh](9602.md), the UK's second-largest financial centre, ranks 29th in the world in the same index.\[^cite_note-[www.longfinance.net-291](http://www.longfinance.net-291/)]&#x20;

The [UK's manufacturing sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manufacturing_in_the_United_Kingdom) in 2024 was the world's [10th-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_manufacturing_output) and Europe's fourth-largest by value output.[^cite_note-292] At the end of 2024 manufacturing in the United Kingdom accounted for 8 per cent of the workforce and 8.6 per cent of national economic output.[^cite_note-293] As reported in 2017 the [East Midlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Midlands_Manufacturing) and [West Midlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Midlands_Manufacturing) (at 12.6 and 11.8 per cent respectively) were the regions with the highest proportion of employees in manufacturing. [London's manufacturing sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London_manufacturing) had the lowest at 2.8 per cent.[^cite_note-294]&#x20;

The [country's tourism sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_the_United_Kingdom) is important to the British economy;[^cite_note-295] The [creative industries](674483.md) accounted for 5.9 per cent of the UK's GVA in 2019,[^cite_note-296] and contributed £111 billion to the UK economy in 2018.[^cite_note-297] [Lloyd's of London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd%27s_of_London), located in London, is the world's largest [insurance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insurance) and [reinsurance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reinsurance) market.[^cite_note-298] [WPP plc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WPP_plc) is one of the world's biggest advertising companies and also based in London.[^cite_note-299] The UK is one of Europe's leading retail markets and its largest [e-commerce](9611.md) market.[^cite_note-300] With [consumption expenditures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consumer_spending) of over US\$2 trillion in 2023, it has the [second-largest consumer market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_consumer_markets) in Europe.[^cite_note-301] [John Lewis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Lewis_Partnership) is the UK's largest employee-owned business.[^cite_note-302]&#x20;

The [British automotive industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automotive_industry_in_the_United_Kingdom) generates £47 billion of exports (12 per cent of the UK's total exports of goods).[^cite_note-Auto_Output_2025-303] In 2024 the UK produced 779,584 passenger vehicles and 125,649 commercial vehicles; 8 out of 10 cars produced in the UK are exported overseas.[^cite_note-Auto_Output_2025-303] Britain is known for iconic cars like [Mini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mini_Hatch) and [Jaguar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaguar_Cars),[^cite_note-304] as well as luxury cars such as [Rolls-Royce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolls-Royce_Motor_Cars), [Bentley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bentley) and [Range Rover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Range_Rover). The UK is a major centre for engine manufacturing: 1.59 million engines were produced in 2024.[^cite_note-Auto_Output_2025-303] It was the world's [third-largest exporter of engines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_engine_exports) by value in 2023.[^cite_note-305] The [UK motorsport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motorsport_in_the_United_Kingdom) industry has an annual turnover of around £10 billion.[^cite_note-306] Seven of the eleven [Formula One](10854.md) teams are based in the UK, with their technology being used in supercars and hypercars from [McLaren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_Automotive), [Aston Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aston_Martin) and [Lotus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lotus_Cars).[^cite_note-307] The [aerospace industry of the UK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerospace_industry_in_the_United_Kingdom) is the second-largest in the world[^cite_note-308] and has an annual turnover of around £30 billion.[^cite_note-309] The [UK space industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_space_programme) was worth £17.5 billion in 2020/21 and employed around 48,800 people.[^cite_note-310][^cite_note-311] The [UK Space Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UK_Space_Agency) has stated in 2023 that it is investing £1.6 billion in space-related projects.[^cite_note-312]&#x20;

The [British agriculture industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_the_United_Kingdom) is intensive, highly mechanised and efficient by European standards, producing approximately 60 per cent of the country's overall food requirements and 73 per cent of its indigenous food needs, using around 0.9 per cent of the labour force (292,000 workers).[^cite_note-313] Around two-thirds of production is devoted to [livestock](25160767.md) and one-third to [arable crops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arable_crops). The UK retains a significant though much reduced fishing industry, with at least 49 per cent of UK fish sustainably caught in 2020.[^cite_note-314] The UK marine natural capital assets were estimated to have a value of £211 billion in 2021.[^cite_note-315] It is rich in a variety of other natural resources including coal, [petroleum](23195.md), [natural gas](22131.md), tin, limestone, iron ore, salt, clay, chalk, [gypsum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gypsum), lead, and silica and has an abundance of arable land.[^cite_note-316]&#x20;

### Science and technology

Main articles: [Science and technology in the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_the_United_Kingdom) and [Telecommunications in the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications_in_the_United_Kingdom)



The [Cambridge Cluster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cambridge_Cluster) is the most intensive research cluster for science and technology in the world.

[^cite_note-317]

England and Scotland were leading centres of the [Scientific Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scientific_Revolution) from the 17th century.[^cite_note-318] The United Kingdom led the [Industrial Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_Revolution) from the 18th century, and has continued to produce scientists and [engineers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_British_engineers) credited with [important advances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_British_innovations_and_discoveries).[^cite_note-319] Major theorists from the 17th and 18th centuries include [Isaac Newton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isaac_Newton), whose [laws of motion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newton%27s_laws_of_motion) and illumination of [gravity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gravitation) have been seen as a keystone of modern science;[^cite_note-320] from the 19th century [Charles Darwin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Darwin), whose theory of [evolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evolution) by [natural selection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_selection) was fundamental to the development of modern biology, and [James Clerk Maxwell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Clerk_Maxwell), who formulated classical [electromagnetic theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electromagnetic_theory); and more recently [Stephen Hawking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Hawking), who advanced major theories in the fields of [cosmology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cosmology), [quantum gravity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quantum_gravity) and the investigation of [black holes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_holes).[^cite_note-321]&#x20;

The [Department for Science, Innovation and Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_for_Science,_Innovation_and_Technology) (DSIT) is responsible for helping to develop and manage the UK's scientific, research, and technological outputs. Scientific research and development remains important in [British universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universities_in_the_United_Kingdom), with many establishing [science parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_park) to facilitate production and co-operation with industry.[^cite_note-322] In 2022, the UK produced [6.0 per cent of the world's scientific research papers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_number_of_scientific_and_technical_journal_articles) and had an 8.8 per cent share of scientific citations, the fourth and third highest among G7 countries and Brazil, China, India, Russia, and South Korea. The UK ranked 1st among these countries for Field-Weighted Citation Impact.[^cite_note-323] Scientific journals produced in the UK include publications by the [Royal Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Society), *[Nature](43427.md)*, the *[British Medical Journal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMJ)* and *[The Lancet](463899.md)*.[^cite_note-324]&#x20;

By 2024 the UK tech sector reached a value of US\$1.2 trillion surpassing the combined values of the French and German sectors.[^cite_note-325] Cambridge was named the number one university in the world for producing successful technology founders.[^cite_note-326] The [UK's artificial intelligence industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_intelligence_industry_in_the_United_Kingdom) is the largest in Europe by value[^cite_note-327] and the country ranked fifth globally in a 2026 report on artificial intelligence development by [Stanford University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanford_University).[^cite_note-328] The UK ranked 6th in the 2025 [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index).[^cite_note-329][^cite_note-330]&#x20;

### Transport

Main article: [Transport in the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_the_United_Kingdom)

Further information: [Transport in England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_England), [Transport in Scotland](2938165.md), [Transport in Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Wales), and [Transport in Northern Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Northern_Ireland)



A high-speed [East Coast Main Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Coast_Main_Line) train in [Northumberland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northumberland), England



A plane taking off from [London City Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London_City_Airport). [London's airports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airports_of_London) make it the city with the [busiest airport system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_busiest_city_airport_systems_by_passenger_traffic) in the world.

In the UK, all traffic [drives on the left](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Left-_and_right-hand_traffic), with distance and speed being measured in miles, yards and miles per hour (mph). All vehicles manufactured for use in the UK have the steering wheel situated on the right side of the vehicle. A radial road network totals 29,145 miles (46,904 km) of main roads, 2,173 miles (3,497 km) of motorways and 213,750 miles (344,000 km) of paved roads.[^cite_note-factbook-147] The [M25](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M25_motorway), encircling London, is the largest and busiest bypass in the world.[^cite_note-331] In 2024 there were 41.7 million licensed vehicles in the UK.[^cite_note-332]&#x20;

The UK has an extensive railway network of 10,072 miles (16,209 km). In Great Britain, the [British Rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Rail) network [was privatised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Privatisation_of_British_Rail) between 1994 and 1997, followed by a rapid rise in passengers. [Great British Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_British_Railways) is a planned state-owned public body that will oversee [rail transport in Great Britain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_Great_Britain). The UK was ranked eighth amongst national European rail systems in the 2017 European Railway Performance Index assessing intensity of use, quality of service and safety.[^cite_note-333]&#x20;

A train runs directly from London to [Paris](22989.md).[^cite_note-334] Called the [Eurostar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurostar), it travels through the [Channel Tunnel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Channel_Tunnel) under the [English Channel](9230.md). At 23.5 miles long, it is the world's longest undersea tunnel.[^cite_note-335] There is also a car service through the tunnel to France called [LeShuttle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LeShuttle). The [Elizabeth line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_line), a rail link running between [East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_London) and [West London](60445975.md), was named in honour of [Queen Elizabeth II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_Elizabeth_II) in 2016 and opened in 2022.[^cite_note-336][^cite_note-337] Another major infrastructure project is [High Speed 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Speed_2) (HS2), a high-speed railway under construction since 2019. It will link London with [Birmingham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birmingham), with the potential to extend further north and capable of speeds of up to 225 mph.[^cite_note-338][^cite_note-339]&#x20;

In 2023 there were 4 billion bus journeys in the UK, 1.8 billion of which were in London.[^cite_note-340] The red [double-decker](902183.md) bus has entered popular culture as an internationally recognised icon of London and England.[^cite_note-341] The [London bus network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London_Buses) is extensive, with over 6,800 scheduled services every weekday carrying about 6 million passengers on over 700 different routes, making it one of the most extensive bus systems in the world and the largest in Europe.[^cite_note-342]&#x20;

During 2024 [British airports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_the_United_Kingdom_and_the_British_Crown_Dependencies) handled nearly 292.5 million passengers.[^cite_note-caastats-343] In that period the three largest airports were [London Heathrow Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London_Heathrow_Airport) (83.9 million passengers), [Gatwick Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gatwick_Airport) (43.2 million passengers) and [Manchester Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manchester_Airport) (30.8 million passengers).[^cite_note-caastats-343] London Heathrow Airport, located 15 miles (24 km) west of the capital, is the world's [second-busiest airport by international passenger traffic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_busiest_airports_by_passenger_traffic) and has the most international passenger traffic of any airport in the world;[^cite_note-344] it is the hub for the UK flag carrier [British Airways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Airways), as well as [Virgin Atlantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virgin_Atlantic).[^cite_note-345] During 2023, 18.3 million passengers travelled internationally by rail and 18.1 million by sea.[^cite_note-346]&#x20;

### Energy

Main article: [Energy in the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_the_United_Kingdom)

Further information: [Energy in England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_England), [Energy in Scotland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_Scotland), [Energy in Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_Wales), and [Energy in Northern Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_Northern_Ireland)

In 2021 the UK was the world's [14th-largest consumer and 22nd-largest producer of energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_energy_consumption_and_production).[^cite_note-United_Kingdom_Energy_Profile-347] It is home to [many large energy companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Six_energy_suppliers), including two of the six major oil and gas companies – [BP](18998720.md) and [Shell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shell_plc).[^cite_note-348]&#x20;



Wind turbines overlooking [Ardrossan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ardrossan) in Scotland. The UK is [a major producer of wind energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wind_power_in_the_United_Kingdom) in Europe.

[^cite_note-349]

Renewable electricity sources provided 51 per cent of the electricity generated in the UK in 2024. [Wind power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wind_power_in_the_United_Kingdom) was the largest source of electricity in 2024, generating 30 per cent of the UK's total electricity.[^cite_note-2024_Energy-350] The UK has the largest offshore wind farm in the world, which is located off the coast of [Yorkshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yorkshire).[^cite_note-351]&#x20;

In 2023 the UK had nine nuclear reactors generating about 15 per cent of the UK's electricity.[^cite_note-coal-ref-352] There are two reactors under construction and more planned.[^cite_note-353][^cite_note-354] In the late 1990s [nuclear power plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_power_plants_in_the_United_Kingdom) contributed around 25 per cent of the total annual electricity generation in the UK, but this has gradually declined as old plants have been shut down. The UK government is investing in [small modular reactors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_modular_reactors) that operate via [nuclear fission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_fission), as well as in research and development towards commercial [fusion reactors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fusion_reactors). To that end the government entered into a partnership with the US in late 2023 to collaborate on fusion technology, with "a commercial grid-ready fusion reactor by 2040" stated as a goal.[^cite_note-355]&#x20;

At the end of 2023 it was estimated that there were 1.1 billion [barrels of oil equivalent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barrels_of_oil_equivalent) of "proven" and "probable" [gas reserves](63420573.md) and 2.3 billion boe of "proven" and "probable" [oil reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Sea_oil) offshore, reducing reliance on imports for energy security and transitioning to renewables.[^cite_note-356] Emissions from UK gas production are roughly four times lower than imported [liquefied natural gas](832128.md) (LNG), according to the [UK's oil and gas regulator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Sea_Transition_Authority).[^cite_note-357]&#x20;

In September 2024 the last coal power station was closed, making [coal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coal) no longer a power source in the UK.[^cite_note-2024_Energy-350] The UK currently has no [fracking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fracking) (hydraulic fracturing) for [shale gas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shale_gas) despite a large supply, due to environmental concerns.[^cite_note-358]&#x20;

### Water supply and sanitation

Main article: [Water supply and sanitation in the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_the_United_Kingdom)

Access to improved water supply and sanitation in the UK is universal. It was estimated that 96 per cent of households were connected to the sewer network in 2022.[^cite_note-359] According to the [Environment Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environment_Agency), total water abstraction for public water supply in the UK was 16,406 [megalitres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megalitre) per day in 2007.[^cite_note-360]&#x20;

In England and Wales, water and sewerage services are provided by 10 private regional water and sewerage companies and 13 mostly smaller private "water only" companies. In Scotland, said services are provided by a single public company, [Scottish Water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scottish_Water). In Northern Ireland, they are also provided by a single public entity, [Northern Ireland Water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Ireland_Water).[^cite_note-361]&#x20;

## Demographics

Main articles: [Demographics of the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_the_United_Kingdom) and [British people](19097669.md)

Further information: [Ceremonial counties of England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceremonial_counties_of_England), [Subdivisions of Scotland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subdivisions_of_Scotland), [Subdivisions of Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subdivisions_of_Wales), and [Counties of Northern Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counties_of_Northern_Ireland)



An elderly British Couple. Approximately 24% of people in England and Wales are aged 60 years and over.

[^cite_note-362]

Whereas the censuses of England and Wales and of Northern Ireland went ahead as planned in 2021, the census in Scotland was delayed until 2022 due to the COVID-19 pandemic. The Office for National Statistics has estimated that the total population of the United Kingdom recorded in the census would have been 66,912,637 had it been conducted in all parts of the UK on 21 March 2021.[^cite_note-Ethnicity2021-7] The UK population is the [fourth-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_European_countries_by_population) in Europe and the [22nd-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_population) in the world. In 2012 and 2013 births contributed the most to population growth, while in 2014 and 2015 net international migration contributed more.[^cite_note-pop2015-363] Between 2001 and 2011 the population increased at an average annual rate of 0.7 per cent.[^cite_note-2011census-364] The [2011 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_United_Kingdom_census) also showed that, over the previous 100 years, the proportion of the population aged 0–14 fell from 31 to 18 per cent, and the proportion of people aged 65 and over rose from 5 to 16 per cent.[^cite_note-2011census-364] In 2018 the [median age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Median_age) of the UK population was 41.7 years.[^cite_note-factbook-147] The 2021 census put Scotland's population at 5.48 million, Wales's at 3.1 million and Northern Ireland's at 1.9 million.[^cite_note-2011census-364]&#x20;

England's population in 2021 was 56 million, representing some 84 per cent of the UK total.[^cite_note-2011census-364] England is one of the [most-densely-populated countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_population_density) in the world, with 434 people per square kilometre in mid-2021,[^cite_note-2011census-364] with a particular concentration in London, the capital,[^cite_note-366][^cite_note-367] and the south-east.[^cite_note-368] [London's wider metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London_metropolitan_area) is the largest in [Western Europe](33800.md), with a population of 14.9 million in 2024.[^cite_note-369]&#x20;

In 2021 the [total fertility rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Total_fertility_rate) across the UK was 1.53 children born per woman,[^cite_note-370] which remains considerably below the [baby boom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mid-20th_century_baby_boom) peak of 2.95 children per woman in 1964,[^cite_note-371] or the high of 6.02 children born per woman in 1815[^cite_note-372] and below the replacement rate of 2.1. In 2011, 47.3 per cent of births in the UK were to unmarried women.[^cite_note-373] The [Office for National Statistics](324029.md) reported in 2015 that amongst the UK population aged 16 and over, 1.7 per cent identify as gay, lesbian, or bisexual (2.0 per cent of males and 1.5 per cent of females); 4.5 per cent of respondents responded with "other", "I don't know", or did not respond.[^cite_note-374] The number of [transgender](19904525.md) people in the UK was estimated to be between 65,000 and 300,000 by research between 2001 and 2008.[^cite_note-375]&#x20;
